    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Heterogeneity Characterization}\n\\label{sec:heterogeneity}\n\nThe benefit of non-uniform allocation depends on how unevenly head sensitivities are distributed.\nWe quantify this with a \\emph{top-5\\% concentration metric}: sort per-head 2-bit distortions $d_h(2)$ in descending order and compute the fraction of total distortion contributed by the top 5\\% of heads.\nHigh concentration indicates that a few heads are disproportionately sensitive; low concentration indicates near-uniform sensitivity.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:heterogeneity} summarizes the results across seven models at $4\\times$ compression.\nThe central finding is that MCKP benefit requires \\emph{spread heterogeneity}---multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients---rather than sensitivity concentrated in a single outlier at one bitwidth.\n\nQwen2.5-7B exemplifies the spread pattern (top-5\\% concentration 0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$), with Layer-0 heads contributing the majority of total 2-bit distortion across multiple bitwidths.\nConstrained MCKP reduces degradation from $+$5.64 PPL (uniform) to $+$1.10 PPL, a 29.4\\% improvement over uniform 4-bit. %exp_id=qwen_constrained_mckp\n\nYi-1.5-9B is a critical counterexample: despite the highest concentration tested ($0.71$, exceeding Qwen's $0.61$), constrained MCKP matches uniform ($+$0.20 vs.\\ $+$0.20). %exp_id=yi15_hires_constrained_mckp\nYi-1.5's sensitivity is dominated by a single extreme outlier: Layer-4 Head-3 ($d_h(2){=}13.12$, second-ranked head: $0.55$, a $24\\times$ gap; $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$). %exp_id=yi15_hires_profiling\nCritically, L4H3's 4-bit and 8-bit deltas are ${\\approx}\\,0$, so upgrading it from 2-bit to 4-bit removes the distortion, but budget freed by downgrading other heads has nowhere useful to go---a ``spike'' pattern with sensitivity at only one bitwidth.\nIn contrast, Qwen's Layer-0 heads exhibit non-zero deltas at multiple bitwidths (e.g., L0H0: 2-bit delta ${=}6.74$, with positive 4-bit and 8-bit deltas), giving the solver a multi-bitwidth gradient across several heads---a ``spread'' pattern enabling meaningful reallocation. %exp_id=qwen_hires_profiling\n\nLlama-3.1-8B shows moderate concentration ($0.34$) with a spike pattern ($t_1/t_5{=}0.71$); its allocation is indistinguishable from uniform ($+$0.36 vs.\\ $+$0.38). %exp_id=llama_hires_bf16_2bitonly %exp_id=llama_hires_constrained\nMistral-7B-v0.3 ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) and Gemma-2-9B ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) both exhibit flat distributions with low concentration ($0.22$ and $0.23$), leaving no reliable signal; constrained MCKP is slightly worse than uniform in both cases ($+$0.13 vs.\\ $+$0.10 for Mistral; $+$0.16 vs.\\ $+$0.14 for Gemma-2). %exp_id=mistral_c4_cross_dataset %exp_id=gemma2_full_pipeline\n\nPythia-1.4B extends the evaluation to MHA (1:1 ratio, 384 heads).\nLow concentration ($0.24$) and $t_1/t_5{=}0.12$ place it in the flat category; constrained MCKP confirms this ($+$10.82 vs.\\ uniform $+$10.66, $+$0.7\\%). %exp_id=pythia14b_constrained_pipeline\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent concentration dilution.}\nQwen2.5-14B (GQA 5:1, 384 heads) provides a within-family comparison: concentration drops from 0.61 (7B) to 0.33 (14B), and $t_1/t_5$ rises from 0.57 to 0.68---a transition from spread to flat.\nSensitivity is dominated by a single head (L0H5, $d_h(2){=}1.845$; second-ranked: $0.237$, a $7.8\\times$ gap), while the $3.4\\times$ increase in head count ($112{\\to}384$) dilutes the remaining signal below the concentration threshold.\nSpread heterogeneity is thus not an intrinsic property of an architecture family but depends on scale: the rule correctly identifies Qwen-7B as the single model$\\times$scale combination where non-uniform allocation helps.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Head sensitivity heterogeneity and constrained allocation benefit at $4\\times$ compression. $t_1/t_5$: ratio of top-1 to top-5 per-head 2-bit distortion (low${=}$spread, high${=}$spike). --- = experiment pending. $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:heterogeneity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & GQA & Top-5\\% & $t_1/t_5$ & $\\Delta$ PPL & $\\Delta$ PPL & Shape \\\\\n & Ratio & Conc. & & (Uniform) & (Ours) & \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B & 7:1 & 0.61 & 0.57 & $+$5.64 & $+$1.10 & Spread \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & 5:1 & 0.33 & 0.57 & --- & --- & Flat \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B & 8:1 & 0.71 & 0.90 & $+$0.20 & $+$0.20 & Spike \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B & 4:1 & 0.34 & 0.71 & $+$0.38 & $+$0.36 & Spike \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 4:1 & 0.22 & 0.18 & $+$0.10 & $+$0.13 & Flat \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$ & 2:1 & 0.23 & 0.18 & $+$0.14 & $+$0.16 & Flat \\\\\nPythia-1.4B & 1:1 & 0.24 & 0.12 & $+$10.66 & $+$10.82 & Flat \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe per-head heatmap (Figure~\\ref{fig:heatmap}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:heatmap_appendix}) confirms this visually: Qwen's Layer-0 heads show high distortion while mid-to-deep layers are near-zero (spread); Yi-1.5 shows a single bright spot at Layer-4 Head-3 (34\\% of total delta) with the remaining 191 heads near-uniform (spike); Gemma-2 and Mistral show uniformly low distortion.\n\n\\subsection{Empirical Decision Rule}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\nMCKP benefit requires not just high concentration but a specific \\emph{shape}---spread heterogeneity across multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients.\nThis motivates a joint empirical decision rule for deciding when non-uniform allocation is worthwhile.\nGiven the profiling results from \\S\\ref{sec:profiling}, compute the top-5\\% concentration and $t_1/t_5$ ratio, then select:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep]\n\\item \\textbf{Spread} (concentration $\\gtrsim 0.50$, $t_1/t_5 \\lesssim 0.70$): non-uniform recommended. Qwen (0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$): $-$29.4\\% PPL.\n\\item \\textbf{Spike} ($t_1/t_5 \\gtrsim 0.70$, concentration $\\gtrsim 0.25$): use uniform. A dominant outlier with no cross-bitwidth gradient cannot be exploited. Yi-1.5 (0.71, 0.90) and Llama (0.34, 0.71): no benefit.\n\\item \\textbf{Flat} (concentration $\\lesssim 0.25$, or remaining moderate-concentration models with $t_1/t_5 \\lesssim 0.70$): use uniform. Too little cumulative delta for meaningful reallocation. Mistral (0.22), Gemma-2 (0.23), Qwen-14B (0.33), and Pythia (0.24): no benefit or counterproductive.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nBoth conditions are needed: Yi-1.5's concentration (0.71) exceeds the 0.50 threshold but its spike shape ($t_1/t_5{=}0.90$) correctly predicts no benefit.\nA concentration-only threshold would misclassify it as spread.\nThe rule correctly classifies all seven evaluated models (7/7), including the within-family scale contrast (Qwen-7B spread vs.\\ 14B flat) and the MHA architecture (Pythia-1.4B flat).\nOnly Qwen-7B exhibits the spread pattern ($N{=}1$); the suggested thresholds should be treated as illustrative until confirmed on additional spread architectures.\nThe rule reuses profiling data already required by the solver and adds no overhead.\n\nAdditional analyses---granularity tradeoff (Appendix~\\ref{sec:granularity}), aggressive compression, profiling resolution and stability, per-layer optimality, and KV separation---are in Appendices~\\ref{sec:granularity}--\\ref{sec:kv_separation}.\n",

constrained MCKP is slightly worse than uniform in both cases ($+$0.13 vs.\\ $+$0.10 for Mistral; $+$0.16 vs.\\ $+$0.14 for Gemma-2). %exp_id=mistral_c4_cross_dataset %exp_id=gemma2_full_pipeline\n\nPythia-1.4B extends the evaluation to MHA (1:1 ratio, 384 heads).\nLow concentration ($0.24$) and $t_1/t_5{=}0.12$ place it in the flat category; constrained MCKP confirms this ($+$10.82 vs.\\ uniform $+$10.66, $+$0.7\\%). %exp_id=pythia14b_constrained_pipeline\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent concentration dilution.}\nQwen2.5-14B (GQA 5:1, 384 heads) provides a within-family comparison: $t_1/t_5$ is identical (0.57), but concentration drops from 0.61 (7B) to 0.33 (14B)---the same within-head structure, diluted by $3.4\\times$ more heads into the flat regime.\nSensitivity is dominated by a single head (L0H5, $d_h(2){=}1.845$; second-ranked: $0.237$, a $7.8\\times$ gap), while the $3.4\\times$ increase in head count ($112{\\to}384$) dilutes the remaining signal below the concentration threshold.\nSpread heterogeneity is thus not an intrinsic property of an architecture family but depends on scale: the rule correctly identifies Qwen-7B as the single model$\\times$scale combination where non-uniform allocation helps.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Head sensitivity heterogeneity and constrained allocation benefit at $4\\times$ compression. $t_1/t_5$: ratio of top-1 to top-5 per-head 2-bit distortion (low${=}$spread, high${=}$spike). --- = experiment pending. $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:heterogeneity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & GQA & Top-5\\% & $t_1/t_5$ & $\\Delta$ PPL & $\\Delta$ PPL & Shape \\\\\n & Ratio & Conc. & & (Uniform) & (Ours) & \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B & 7:1 & 0.61 & 0.57 & $+$5.64 & $+$1.10 & Spread \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & 5:1 & 0.33 & 0.57 & --- & --- & Flat \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B & 8:1 & 0.71 & 0.90 & $+$0.20 & $+$0.20 & Spike \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B & 4:1 & 0.34 & 0.71 & $+$0.38 & $+$0.36 & Spike \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 4:1 & 0.22 & 0.18 & $+$0.10 & $+$0.13 & Flat \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$ & 2:1 & 0.23 & 0.18 & $+$0.14 & $+$0.16 & Flat \\\\\nPythia-1.4B & 1:1 & 0.24 & 0.12 & $+$10.66 & $+$10.82 & Flat \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe per-head heatmap (Figure~\\ref{fig:heatmap}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:heatmap_appendix}) confirms this visually: Qwen's Layer-0 heads show high distortion while mid-to-deep layers are near-zero (spread); Yi-1.5 shows a single bright spot at Layer-4 Head-3 (34\\% of total delta) with the remaining 191 heads near-uniform (spike); Gemma-2 and Mistral show uniformly low distortion.\n\n\\subsection{Empirical Decision Rule}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\nMCKP benefit requires not just high concentration but a specific \\emph{shape}---spread heterogeneity across multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients.\nThis motivates a joint empirical decision rule for deciding when non-uniform allocation is worthwhile.\nGiven the profiling results from \\S\\ref{sec:profiling}, compute the top-5\\% concentration and $t_1/t_5$ ratio, then select:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep]\n\\item \\textbf{Spread} (concentration $\\gtrsim 0.50$, $t_1/t_5 \\lesssim 0.70$): non-uniform recommended. Qwen (0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$): $-$29.4\\% PPL.\n\\item \\textbf{Spike} ($t_1/t_5 \\gtrsim 0.70$, concentration $\\gtrsim 0.25$): use uniform. A dominant outlier with no cross-bitwidth gradient cannot be exploited. Yi-1.5 (0.71, 0.90) and Llama (0.34, 0.71): no benefit.\n\\item \\textbf{Flat} (concentration $\\lesssim 0.25$, or remaining moderate-concentration models with $t_1/t_5 \\lesssim 0.70$): use uniform. Too little cumulative delta for meaningful reallocation. Mistral (0.22), Gemma-2 (0.23), Qwen-14B (0.33), and Pythia (0.24): no benefit or counterproductive.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nBoth conditions are needed: Yi-1.5's concentration (0.71) exceeds the 0.50 threshold but its spike shape ($t_1/t_5{=}0.90$) correctly predicts no benefit.\nA concentration-only threshold would misclassify it as spread.\nThe rule correctly classifies all seven evaluated models (7/7), including the within-family scale contrast (Qwen-7B spread vs.\\ 14B flat) and the MHA architecture (Pythia-1.4B flat).\nOnly Qwen-7B exhibits the spread pattern ($N{=}1$); the suggested thresholds should be treated as illustrative until confirmed on additional spread architectures.\nThe rule reuses profiling data already required by the solver and adds no overhead.\n\nAdditional analyses---granularity tradeoff (Appendix~\\ref{sec:granularity}), aggressive compression, profiling resolution and stability, per-layer optimality, and KV separation---are in Appendices~\\ref{sec:granularity}--\\ref{sec:kv_separation}.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\n\\paragraph{Models.}\nWe evaluate seven models spanning five architecture families and diverse attention configurations:\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-7B} (GQA 7:1, 28 layers $\\times$ 4 KV heads = 112),\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-14B} (GQA 5:1, 48 $\\times$ 8 = 384),\n\\textbf{Yi-1.5-9B} (GQA 8:1, 48 $\\times$ 4 = 192),\n\\textbf{Llama-3.1-8B} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256),\n\\textbf{Mistral-7B-v0.3} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256, sliding window attention),\n\\textbf{Gemma-2-9B} (GQA 2:1, 42 $\\times$ 8 = 336, \\texttt{head\\_dim}=256, attention logit softcapping),\nand \\textbf{Pythia-1.4B} (MHA 1:1, 24 $\\times$ 16 = 384).\nThis selection spans GQA ratios from 1:1 (MHA) to 8:1 and covers standard, sliding-window, softcapped, and multi-head attention.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation.}\nWe report perplexity (PPL, $\\downarrow$) on the WikiText-2 test split (298{,}938 tokens) under bf16 inference with stride 512.\nFor downstream evaluation, we use ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, PIQA, and WinoGrande under 0-shot prompting.\n\n\\paragraph{Profiling.}\nPer-head sensitivity is measured by independently quantizing each head to each candidate bit-width in $\\{2, 4, 8, 16\\}$ and recording the PPL increase over the bf16 baseline.\nAll results use high-resolution profiling (64 sequences $\\times$ 2048 tokens from WikiText-2 train); Appendix~\\ref{sec:profiling_resolution} compares against standard-resolution profiling (16 seq $\\times$ 512 tok).\n\n\\paragraph{Compression and constraint.}\nThe default target is 4$\\times$ compression (average 4 bits, budget ratio 0.25).\nThe cardinality constraint limits 2-bit assignments to at most $k$ heads, where $k$ is selected via the compounding sweep described in \\S\\ref{sec:method} ($k{=}8$ for Qwen-7B, $k{=}2$ for Yi-1.5 and Llama, $k{=}4$ for Mistral, $k{=}8$ for Gemma-2 and Pythia; Qwen-14B $k$ value pending).\n\n\\paragraph{Baselines.}\nWe compare against:\n(1)~uniform 4-bit quantization;\n(2)~unconstrained greedy allocation (RateQuant-style~\\cite{zuo2026ratequant});\n(3)~unconstrained MCKP allocation;\n(4)~an L0-random heuristic that assigns Layer-0 heads to 8-bit and $k$ random non-Layer-0 heads to 2-bit, with remaining heads at 4-bit (5 seeds; we report mean$\\pm$std and median);\nand (5)~a naive positional heuristic that assigns Layer-0 to 8-bit and the $k$ deepest heads to 2-bit.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main_results}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{PPL ($\\downarrow$) on WikiText-2 at 4$\\times$ compression. Unconstrained failure: catastrophic on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), severe on Pythia ($2.3\\times$), moderate on Gemma-2 ($1.5\\times$), mild on remaining (${\\sim}1.1\\times$). Greedy and MCKP produce identical unconstrained allocations on 4/5 models. Best constrained result per model in \\textbf{bold}; --- = experiment pending. $^\\ddagger$Best $k$ from ablation ($k{=}4$). $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & Baseline & Uniform & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Unconstrained} & Ours \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){4-5}\n & (bf16) & (4-bit) & Greedy & MCKP & (Constr.) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B      & 9.81  & 15.45 & 32.45 & 36.92 & \\textbf{10.91} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B     & 5.84  & ---   & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B       & 7.02  & 7.22  & 7.56  & 7.56  & 7.22 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B    & 8.09  & 8.47  & 8.63  & 8.63  & \\textbf{8.45} \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 7.04  & 7.13  & 7.57  & 7.60  & 7.16$^\\ddagger$ \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$  & 6.03  & 6.17  & 8.85  & 8.85  & 6.20 \\\\\nPythia-1.4B     & 13.63 & 24.29 & ---   & 31.54 & 24.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen2.5-7B, constrained MCKP achieves 10.91 PPL, a 29.4\\% reduction over uniform 4-bit (15.45).\nWithout the constraint, both greedy (32.45) and MCKP (36.92) perform \\emph{worse} than uniform---a catastrophic failure from excessive 2-bit assignments compounding across layers.\nAcross 3 calibration seeds, constrained MCKP yields mean PPL 10.99$\\pm$0.06 with 90.2\\% three-way allocation agreement, confirming profiling stability.\n\nOn Llama-3.1-8B, the result is essentially indistinguishable from uniform (8.45 vs.\\ 8.47, $\\Delta < 0.02$ PPL).\nHires profiling reveals a spike pattern (concentration 0.34, $t_1/t_5{=}0.71$) dominated by a single top head (L2H5), leaving little cross-bitwidth gradient for non-uniform allocation to exploit.\n\nOn Yi-1.5-9B, constrained MCKP ($k{=}2$) matches uniform (7.22); despite the highest concentration tested (0.71), sensitivity is dominated by a single outlier (L4H3, $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$), a spike pattern with no cross-bitwidth gradient.\nOn Mistral-7B-v0.3, constrained MCKP (7.16, $k{=}4$) is slightly worse than uniform (7.13, $+$0.4\\%); near-zero sensitivity differences and sliding-window attention may both contribute.\n\nOn Gemma-2-9B$^*$ (GQA 2:1), constrained MCKP ($k{=}8$) achieves 6.20 PPL vs.\\ uniform 6.17 ($+$0.4\\%).\nDespite flat sensitivity (concentration 0.23), unconstrained MCKP degrades to 8.85 ($1.47\\times$ bf16) from 31.5\\% 2-bit over-allocation; the constraint reduces this to 8 heads, confirming universal over-allocation prevention.\n\nUnconstrained failure severity tracks heterogeneity shape: \\emph{catastrophic} on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), \\emph{severe} on Pythia ($2.3\\times$, 90/384 heads at 2-bit), \\emph{moderate} on Gemma-2 ($1.47\\times$, 31.5\\% 2-bit), and \\emph{mild} on the remaining models (${\\sim}1.07$--$1.08\\times$).\nSee Appendix~\\ref{sec:allocation_appendix} (allocation visualization) and Appendix~\\ref{sec:budget_sweep} (budget sweep) for additional detail.\n\nMCKP consistently outperforms random L0-constrained allocation and naive positional baselines across all three tested models (median gap 5--16\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:heuristic} in Appendix~\\ref{sec:heuristic_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Comparison with KIVI.}\nKIVI~\\cite{liu2024kivi} uses asymmetric per-channel quantization with per-token scaling, applied uniformly across all heads.\nAt 4-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI achieves near-lossless PPL (9.91 on Qwen, 8.14 on Llama vs.\\ 9.81/8.09 bf16), while our symmetric scheme yields 15.45 and 8.47---a gap from the quantization method, not allocation.\nHowever, KIVI cannot exploit head-level heterogeneity: on Qwen (spread), constrained MCKP reduces symmetric 4-bit from 15.45 to 10.91 ($-$4.54 PPL) by reallocating bits across heads, a degree of freedom unavailable to uniform-precision methods; on Llama (spike), the gain is negligible ($-$0.02).\nAt 2-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI degrades further (13.34 on Qwen, 10.08 on Llama) as sensitive heads receive the same precision as insensitive ones.\nThe two approaches are orthogonal and composable: KIVI selects the per-channel scheme, our method selects the per-head bit-width.\n\n\\subsection{Downstream Evaluation}\n\\label{sec:downstream}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{0-shot accuracy change (Ours $-$ Uniform, pts) at 4$\\times$ compression. Qwen shows large gains; the remaining models show small degradations or neutrality, tracking PPL results.}\n\\label{tab:downstream}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & ARC-C & HS & PIQA & WG & Avg \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B        & $+$3.2 & $+$4.0 & $+$1.3 & $+$2.0 & $+$2.6 \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B        & $-$3.2 & $-$0.6 & $-$0.3 & $+$1.3 & $-$0.8 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B     & $-$2.7 & $-$0.1 & $-$0.7 & $-$0.2 & $-$1.0 \\\\\nMistral-7B       & $-$1.2 & $-$0.2 & $+$0.4 & $-$1.0 & $-$0.5 \\\\\nGemma-2-9B       & $-$0.2 & $+$0.3 & $-$0.1 & $+$0.8 & $+$0.1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen, constrained MCKP improves average 0-shot accuracy by 2.6 pts over uniform (0.686 vs.\\ 0.660), recovering $\\sim$73\\% of the accuracy lost to quantization.\nThe four non-spread architectures show minor average degradation (Yi $-$0.8, Llama $-$1.0, Mistral $-$0.5, Gemma-2 $+$0.1 pts); Llama is the most systematic, with 0 of 5 tasks showing positive change.\nARC-Challenge shows the largest per-task sensitivity across all models (Yi $-$3.2, Llama $-$2.7, Mistral $-$1.2, Gemma-2 $-$0.2 pts), consistent with multi-step reasoning where KV cache errors accumulate across longer inference chains.\nCross-dataset stability analysis (Appendix~\\ref{sec:cross_dataset}) confirms ${>}96\\%$ bit-assignment agreement between WikiText-2 and C4 calibration across all three tested models.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\n\\paragraph{Models.}\nWe evaluate seven models spanning five architecture families and diverse attention configurations:\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-7B} (GQA 7:1, 28 layers $\\times$ 4 KV heads = 112),\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-14B} (GQA 5:1, 48 $\\times$ 8 = 384),\n\\textbf{Yi-1.5-9B} (GQA 8:1, 48 $\\times$ 4 = 192),\n\\textbf{Llama-3.1-8B} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256),\n\\textbf{Mistral-7B-v0.3} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256, sliding window attention),\n\\textbf{Gemma-2-9B} (GQA 2:1, 42 $\\times$ 8 = 336, \\texttt{head\\_dim}=256, attention logit softcapping),\nand \\textbf{Pythia-1.4B} (MHA 1:1, 24 $\\times$ 16 = 384).\nThis selection spans GQA ratios from 1:1 (MHA) to 8:1 and covers standard, sliding-window, softcapped, and multi-head attention.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation.}\nWe report perplexity (PPL, $\\downarrow$) on the WikiText-2 test split (298{,}938 tokens) under bf16 inference with stride 512.\nFor downstream evaluation, we use ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, PIQA, and WinoGrande under 0-shot prompting.\n\n\\paragraph{Profiling.}\nPer-head sensitivity is measured by independently quantizing each head to each candidate bit-width in $\\{2, 4, 8, 16\\}$ and recording the PPL increase over the bf16 baseline.\nAll results use high-resolution profiling (64 sequences $\\times$ 2048 tokens from WikiText-2 train); Appendix~\\ref{sec:profiling_resolution} compares against standard-resolution profiling (16 seq $\\times$ 512 tok).\n\n\\paragraph{Compression and constraint.}\nThe default target is 4$\\times$ compression (average 4 bits, budget ratio 0.25).\nThe cardinality constraint limits 2-bit assignments to at most $k$ heads, where $k$ is selected via the compounding sweep described in \\S\\ref{sec:method} ($k{=}8$ for Qwen-7B, $k{=}2$ for Yi-1.5 and Llama, $k{=}4$ for Mistral, $k{=}8$ for Gemma-2 and Pythia; Qwen-14B $k$ value pending).\n\n\\paragraph{Baselines.}\nWe compare against:\n(1)~uniform 4-bit quantization;\n(2)~unconstrained greedy allocation (RateQuant-style~\\cite{zuo2026ratequant});\n(3)~unconstrained MCKP allocation;\n(4)~an L0-random heuristic that assigns Layer-0 heads to 8-bit and $k$ random non-Layer-0 heads to 2-bit, with remaining heads at 4-bit (5 seeds; we report mean$\\pm$std and median);\nand (5)~a naive positional heuristic that assigns Layer-0 to 8-bit and the $k$ deepest heads to 2-bit.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main_results}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{PPL ($\\downarrow$) on WikiText-2 at 4$\\times$ compression. Unconstrained failure: catastrophic on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), severe on Pythia ($2.3\\times$), moderate on Gemma-2 ($1.5\\times$), mild on remaining (${\\sim}1.1\\times$). Greedy and MCKP produce identical unconstrained allocations on 4/5 models. Best constrained result per model in \\textbf{bold}; --- = experiment pending. $^\\ddagger$Best $k$ from ablation ($k{=}4$). $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & Baseline & Uniform & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Unconstrained} & Ours \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){4-5}\n & (bf16) & (4-bit) & Greedy & MCKP & (Constr.) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B      & 9.81  & 15.45 & 32.45 & 36.92 & \\textbf{10.91} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B     & 5.84  & ---   & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B       & 7.02  & 7.22  & 7.56  & 7.56  & 7.22 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B    & 8.09  & 8.47  & 8.63  & 8.63  & \\textbf{8.45} \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 7.04  & 7.13  & 7.57  & 7.60  & 7.16$^\\ddagger$ \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$  & 6.03  & 6.17  & 8.85  & 8.85  & 6.20 \\\\\nPythia-1.4B     & 16.49 & 24.29 & ---   & 31.54 & 24.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen2.5-7B, constrained MCKP achieves 10.91 PPL, a 29.4\\% reduction over uniform 4-bit (15.45).\nWithout the constraint, both greedy (32.45) and MCKP (36.92) perform \\emph{worse} than uniform---a catastrophic failure from excessive 2-bit assignments compounding across layers.\nAcross 3 calibration seeds, constrained MCKP yields mean PPL 10.99$\\pm$0.06 with 90.2\\% three-way allocation agreement, confirming profiling stability.\n\nOn Llama-3.1-8B, the result is essentially indistinguishable from uniform (8.45 vs.\\ 8.47, $\\Delta < 0.02$ PPL).\nHires profiling reveals a spike pattern (concentration 0.34, $t_1/t_5{=}0.71$) dominated by a single top head (L2H5), leaving little cross-bitwidth gradient for non-uniform allocation to exploit.\n\nOn Yi-1.5-9B, constrained MCKP ($k{=}2$) matches uniform (7.22); despite the highest concentration tested (0.71), sensitivity is dominated by a single outlier (L4H3, $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$), a spike pattern with no cross-bitwidth gradient.\nOn Mistral-7B-v0.3, constrained MCKP (7.16, $k{=}4$) is slightly worse than uniform (7.13, $+$0.4\\%); near-zero sensitivity differences and sliding-window attention may both contribute.\n\nOn Gemma-2-9B$^*$ (GQA 2:1), constrained MCKP ($k{=}8$) achieves 6.20 PPL vs.\\ uniform 6.17 ($+$0.4\\%).\nDespite flat sensitivity (concentration 0.23), unconstrained MCKP degrades to 8.85 ($1.47\\times$ bf16) from 31.5\\% 2-bit over-allocation; the constraint reduces this to 8 heads, confirming universal over-allocation prevention.\n\nUnconstrained failure severity tracks heterogeneity shape: \\emph{catastrophic} on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), \\emph{severe} on Pythia ($2.3\\times$, 90/384 heads at 2-bit), \\emph{moderate} on Gemma-2 ($1.47\\times$, 31.5\\% 2-bit), and \\emph{mild} on the remaining models (${\\sim}1.07$--$1.08\\times$).\nSee Appendix~\\ref{sec:allocation_appendix} (allocation visualization) and Appendix~\\ref{sec:budget_sweep} (budget sweep) for additional detail.\n\nMCKP consistently outperforms random L0-constrained allocation and naive positional baselines across all three tested models (median gap 5--16\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:heuristic} in Appendix~\\ref{sec:heuristic_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Comparison with KIVI.}\nKIVI~\\cite{liu2024kivi} uses asymmetric per-channel quantization with per-token scaling, applied uniformly across all heads.\nAt 4-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI achieves near-lossless PPL (9.91 on Qwen, 8.14 on Llama vs.\\ 9.81/8.09 bf16), while our symmetric scheme yields 15.45 and 8.47---a gap from the quantization method, not allocation.\nHowever, KIVI cannot exploit head-level heterogeneity: on Qwen (spread), constrained MCKP reduces symmetric 4-bit from 15.45 to 10.91 ($-$4.54 PPL) by reallocating bits across heads, a degree of freedom unavailable to uniform-precision methods; on Llama (spike), the gain is negligible ($-$0.02).\nAt 2-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI degrades further (13.34 on Qwen, 10.08 on Llama) as sensitive heads receive the same precision as insensitive ones.\nThe two approaches are orthogonal and composable: KIVI selects the per-channel scheme, our method selects the per-head bit-width.\n\n\\subsection{Downstream Evaluation}\n\\label{sec:downstream}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{0-shot accuracy change (Ours $-$ Uniform, pts) at 4$\\times$ compression. Qwen shows large gains; the remaining models show small degradations or neutrality, tracking PPL results.}\n\\label{tab:downstream}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & ARC-C & HS & PIQA & WG & Avg \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B        & $+$3.2 & $+$4.0 & $+$1.3 & $+$2.0 & $+$2.6 \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B        & $-$3.2 & $-$0.6 & $-$0.3 & $+$1.3 & $-$0.8 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B     & $-$2.7 & $-$0.1 & $-$0.7 & $-$0.2 & $-$1.0 \\\\\nMistral-7B       & $-$1.2 & $-$0.2 & $+$0.4 & $-$1.0 & $-$0.5 \\\\\nGemma-2-9B       & $-$0.2 & $+$0.3 & $-$0.1 & $+$0.8 & $+$0.1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen, constrained MCKP improves average 0-shot accuracy by 2.6 pts over uniform (0.686 vs.\\ 0.660), recovering $\\sim$73\\% of the accuracy lost to quantization.\nThe four non-spread architectures show minor average degradation (Yi $-$0.8, Llama $-$1.0, Mistral $-$0.5, Gemma-2 $+$0.1 pts); Llama is the most systematic, with 0 of 5 tasks showing positive change.\nARC-Challenge shows the largest per-task sensitivity across all models (Yi $-$3.2, Llama $-$2.7, Mistral $-$1.2, Gemma-2 $-$0.2 pts), consistent with multi-step reasoning where KV cache errors accumulate across longer inference chains.\nCross-dataset stability analysis (Appendix~\\ref{sec:cross_dataset}) confirms ${>}96\\%$ bit-assignment agreement between WikiText-2 and C4 calibration across all three tested models.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\n\\paragraph{Models.}\nWe evaluate seven models spanning five architecture families and diverse attention configurations:\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-7B} (GQA 7:1, 28 layers $\\times$ 4 KV heads = 112),\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-14B} (GQA 5:1, 48 $\\times$ 8 = 384),\n\\textbf{Yi-1.5-9B} (GQA 8:1, 48 $\\times$ 4 = 192),\n\\textbf{Llama-3.1-8B} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256),\n\\textbf{Mistral-7B-v0.3} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256, sliding window attention),\n\\textbf{Gemma-2-9B} (GQA 2:1, 42 $\\times$ 8 = 336, \\texttt{head\\_dim}=256, attention logit softcapping),\nand \\textbf{Pythia-1.4B} (MHA 1:1, 24 $\\times$ 16 = 384).\nThis selection spans GQA ratios from 1:1 (MHA) to 8:1 and covers standard, sliding-window, softcapped, and multi-head attention.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation.}\nWe report perplexity (PPL, $\\downarrow$) on the WikiText-2 test split (298{,}938 tokens) under bf16 inference with stride 512.\nFor downstream evaluation, we use ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, PIQA, and WinoGrande under 0-shot prompting.\n\n\\paragraph{Profiling.}\nPer-head sensitivity is measured by independently quantizing each head to each candidate bit-width in $\\{2, 4, 8, 16\\}$ and recording the PPL increase over the bf16 baseline.\nAll results use high-resolution profiling (64 sequences $\\times$ 2048 tokens from WikiText-2 train); Appendix~\\ref{sec:profiling_resolution} compares against standard-resolution profiling (16 seq $\\times$ 512 tok).\n\n\\paragraph{Compression and constraint.}\nThe default target is 4$\\times$ compression (average 4 bits, budget ratio 0.25).\nThe cardinality constraint limits 2-bit assignments to at most $k$ heads, where $k$ is selected via the compounding sweep described in \\S\\ref{sec:method} ($k{=}8$ for Qwen-7B, $k{=}2$ for Yi-1.5 and Llama, $k{=}4$ for Mistral, $k{=}8$ for Gemma-2 and Pythia; Qwen-14B $k$ value pending).\n\n\\paragraph{Baselines.}\nWe compare against:\n(1)~uniform 4-bit quantization;\n(2)~unconstrained greedy allocation (RateQuant-style~\\cite{zuo2026ratequant});\n(3)~unconstrained MCKP allocation;\n(4)~an L0-random heuristic that assigns Layer-0 heads to 8-bit and $k$ random non-Layer-0 heads to 2-bit, with remaining heads at 4-bit (5 seeds; we report mean$\\pm$std and median);\nand (5)~a naive positional heuristic that assigns Layer-0 to 8-bit and the $k$ deepest heads to 2-bit.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main_results}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{PPL ($\\downarrow$) on WikiText-2 at 4$\\times$ compression. Unconstrained failure: catastrophic on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), severe on Pythia ($1.9\\times$), moderate on Gemma-2 ($1.5\\times$), mild on remaining (${\\sim}1.1\\times$). Greedy and MCKP produce identical unconstrained allocations on 4/5 models. Best constrained result per model in \\textbf{bold}; --- = experiment pending. $^\\ddagger$Best $k$ from ablation ($k{=}4$). $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & Baseline & Uniform & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Unconstrained} & Ours \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){4-5}\n & (bf16) & (4-bit) & Greedy & MCKP & (Constr.) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B      & 9.81  & 15.45 & 32.45 & 36.92 & \\textbf{10.91} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B     & 5.84  & ---   & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B       & 7.02  & 7.22  & 7.56  & 7.56  & 7.22 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B    & 8.09  & 8.47  & 8.63  & 8.63  & \\textbf{8.45} \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 7.04  & 7.13  & 7.57  & 7.60  & 7.16$^\\ddagger$ \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$  & 6.03  & 6.17  & 8.85  & 8.85  & 6.20 \\\\\nPythia-1.4B     & 16.49 & 24.29 & ---   & 31.54 & 24.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen2.5-7B, constrained MCKP achieves 10.91 PPL, a 29.4\\% reduction over uniform 4-bit (15.45).\nWithout the constraint, both greedy (32.45) and MCKP (36.92) perform \\emph{worse} than uniform---a catastrophic failure from excessive 2-bit assignments compounding across layers.\nAcross 3 calibration seeds, constrained MCKP yields mean PPL 10.99$\\pm$0.06 with 90.2\\% three-way allocation agreement, confirming profiling stability.\n\nOn Llama-3.1-8B, the result is essentially indistinguishable from uniform (8.45 vs.\\ 8.47, $\\Delta < 0.02$ PPL).\nHires profiling reveals a spike pattern (concentration 0.34, $t_1/t_5{=}0.71$) dominated by a single top head (L2H5), leaving little cross-bitwidth gradient for non-uniform allocation to exploit.\n\nOn Yi-1.5-9B, constrained MCKP ($k{=}2$) matches uniform (7.22); despite the highest concentration tested (0.71), sensitivity is dominated by a single outlier (L4H3, $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$), a spike pattern with no cross-bitwidth gradient.\nOn Mistral-7B-v0.3, constrained MCKP (7.16, $k{=}4$) is slightly worse than uniform (7.13, $+$0.4\\%); near-zero sensitivity differences and sliding-window attention may both contribute.\n\nOn Gemma-2-9B$^*$ (GQA 2:1), constrained MCKP ($k{=}8$) achieves 6.20 PPL vs.\\ uniform 6.17 ($+$0.4\\%).\nDespite flat sensitivity (concentration 0.23), unconstrained MCKP degrades to 8.85 ($1.47\\times$ bf16) from 31.5\\% 2-bit over-allocation; the constraint reduces this to 8 heads, confirming universal over-allocation prevention.\n\nUnconstrained failure severity tracks heterogeneity shape: \\emph{catastrophic} on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), \\emph{severe} on Pythia ($2.3\\times$, 90/384 heads at 2-bit), \\emph{moderate} on Gemma-2 ($1.47\\times$, 31.5\\% 2-bit), and \\emph{mild} on the remaining models (${\\sim}1.07$--$1.08\\times$).\nSee Appendix~\\ref{sec:allocation_appendix} (allocation visualization) and Appendix~\\ref{sec:budget_sweep} (budget sweep) for additional detail.\n\nMCKP consistently outperforms random L0-constrained allocation and naive positional baselines across all three tested models (median gap 5--16\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:heuristic} in Appendix~\\ref{sec:heuristic_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Comparison with KIVI.}\nKIVI~\\cite{liu2024kivi} uses asymmetric per-channel quantization with per-token scaling, applied uniformly across all heads.\nAt 4-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI achieves near-lossless PPL (9.91 on Qwen, 8.14 on Llama vs.\\ 9.81/8.09 bf16), while our symmetric scheme yields 15.45 and 8.47---a gap from the quantization method, not allocation.\nHowever, KIVI cannot exploit head-level heterogeneity: on Qwen (spread), constrained MCKP reduces symmetric 4-bit from 15.45 to 10.91 ($-$4.54 PPL) by reallocating bits across heads, a degree of freedom unavailable to uniform-precision methods; on Llama (spike), the gain is negligible ($-$0.02).\nAt 2-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI degrades further (13.34 on Qwen, 10.08 on Llama) as sensitive heads receive the same precision as insensitive ones.\nThe two approaches are orthogonal and composable: KIVI selects the per-channel scheme, our method selects the per-head bit-width.\n\n\\subsection{Downstream Evaluation}\n\\label{sec:downstream}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{0-shot accuracy change (Ours $-$ Uniform, pts) at 4$\\times$ compression. Qwen shows large gains; the remaining models show small degradations or neutrality, tracking PPL results.}\n\\label{tab:downstream}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & ARC-C & HS & PIQA & WG & Avg \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B        & $+$3.2 & $+$4.0 & $+$1.3 & $+$2.0 & $+$2.6 \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B        & $-$3.2 & $-$0.6 & $-$0.3 & $+$1.3 & $-$0.8 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B     & $-$2.7 & $-$0.1 & $-$0.7 & $-$0.2 & $-$1.0 \\\\\nMistral-7B       & $-$1.2 & $-$0.2 & $+$0.4 & $-$1.0 & $-$0.5 \\\\\nGemma-2-9B       & $-$0.2 & $+$0.3 & $-$0.1 & $+$0.8 & $+$0.1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen, constrained MCKP improves average 0-shot accuracy by 2.6 pts over uniform (0.686 vs.\\ 0.660), recovering $\\sim$73\\% of the accuracy lost to quantization.\nThe four non-spread architectures show minor average degradation (Yi $-$0.8, Llama $-$1.0, Mistral $-$0.5, Gemma-2 $+$0.1 pts); Llama is the most systematic, with 0 of 5 tasks showing positive change.\nARC-Challenge shows the largest per-task sensitivity across all models (Yi $-$3.2, Llama $-$2.7, Mistral $-$1.2, Gemma-2 $-$0.2 pts), consistent with multi-step reasoning where KV cache errors accumulate across longer inference chains.\nCross-dataset stability analysis (Appendix~\\ref{sec:cross_dataset}) confirms ${>}96\\%$ bit-assignment agreement between WikiText-2 and C4 calibration across all three tested models.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Heterogeneity Characterization}\n\\label{sec:heterogeneity}\n\nThe benefit of non-uniform allocation depends on how unevenly head sensitivities are distributed.\nWe quantify this with a \\emph{top-5\\% concentration metric}: sort per-head 2-bit distortions $d_h(2)$ in descending order and compute the fraction of total distortion contributed by the top 5\\% of heads.\nHigh concentration indicates that a few heads are disproportionately sensitive; low concentration indicates near-uniform sensitivity.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:heterogeneity} summarizes the results across seven models at $4\\times$ compression.\nThe central finding is that MCKP benefit requires \\emph{spread heterogeneity}---multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients---rather than sensitivity concentrated in a single outlier at one bitwidth.\n\nQwen2.5-7B exemplifies the spread pattern (top-5\\% concentration 0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$), with Layer-0 heads contributing the majority of total 2-bit distortion across multiple bitwidths.\nConstrained MCKP reduces degradation from $+$5.64 PPL (uniform) to $+$1.10 PPL, a 29.4\\% improvement over uniform 4-bit. %exp_id=qwen_constrained_mckp\n\nYi-1.5-9B is a critical counterexample: despite the highest concentration tested ($0.71$, exceeding Qwen's $0.61$), constrained MCKP matches uniform ($+$0.20 vs.\\ $+$0.20). %exp_id=yi15_hires_constrained_mckp\nYi-1.5's sensitivity is dominated by a single extreme outlier: Layer-4 Head-3 ($d_h(2){=}13.12$, second-ranked head: $0.55$, a $24\\times$ gap; $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$). %exp_id=yi15_hires_profiling\nCritically, L4H3's 4-bit and 8-bit deltas are ${\\approx}\\,0$, so upgrading it from 2-bit to 4-bit removes the distortion, but budget freed by downgrading other heads has nowhere useful to go---a ``spike'' pattern with sensitivity at only one bitwidth.\nIn contrast, Qwen's Layer-0 heads exhibit non-zero deltas at multiple bitwidths (e.g., L0H0: 2-bit delta ${=}6.74$, with positive 4-bit and 8-bit deltas), giving the solver a multi-bitwidth gradient across several heads---a ``spread'' pattern enabling meaningful reallocation. %exp_id=qwen_hires_profiling\n\nLlama-3.1-8B shows moderate concentration ($0.34$) with a spike pattern ($t_1/t_5{=}0.71$); its allocation is indistinguishable from uniform ($+$0.36 vs.\\ $+$0.38). %exp_id=llama_hires_bf16_2bitonly %exp_id=llama_hires_constrained\nMistral-7B-v0.3 ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) and Gemma-2-9B ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) both exhibit flat distributions with low concentration ($0.22$ and $0.23$), leaving no reliable signal; constrained MCKP is slightly worse than uniform in both cases ($+$0.13 vs.\\ $+$0.10 for Mistral; $+$0.16 vs.\\ $+$0.14 for Gemma-2). %exp_id=mistral_c4_cross_dataset %exp_id=gemma2_full_pipeline\n\nPythia-1.4B extends the evaluation to MHA (1:1 ratio, 384 heads).\nLow concentration ($0.24$) and $t_1/t_5{=}0.12$ place it in the flat category; constrained MCKP confirms this ($+$10.82 vs.\\ uniform $+$10.66, $+$0.7\\%). %exp_id=pythia14b_constrained_pipeline\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent concentration dilution.}\nQwen2.5-14B (GQA 5:1, 384 heads) provides a within-family comparison: $t_1/t_5$ is identical (0.57), but concentration drops from 0.61 (7B) to 0.33 (14B)---the same within-head structure, diluted by $3.4\\times$ more heads into the flat regime.\nSensitivity is dominated by a single head (L0H5, $d_h(2){=}1.845$; second-ranked: $0.237$, a $7.8\\times$ gap), but 384 heads dilute cumulative signal below the concentration threshold.\nConcentration, not within-head structure, is thus the primary discriminator: the rule correctly identifies Qwen-7B as the single model$\\times$scale combination where non-uniform allocation helps.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Head sensitivity heterogeneity and constrained allocation benefit at $4\\times$ compression. $t_1/t_5$: ratio of top-1 to top-5 per-head 2-bit distortion (low${=}$spread, high${=}$spike). --- = experiment pending. $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:heterogeneity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & GQA & Top-5\\% & $t_1/t_5$ & $\\Delta$ PPL & $\\Delta$ PPL & Shape \\\\\n & Ratio & Conc. & & (Uniform) & (Ours) & \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B & 7:1 & 0.61 & 0.57 & $+$5.64 & $+$1.10 & Spread \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & 5:1 & 0.33 & 0.57 & --- & --- & Flat \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B & 8:1 & 0.71 & 0.90 & $+$0.20 & $+$0.20 & Spike \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B & 4:1 & 0.34 & 0.71 & $+$0.38 & $+$0.36 & Spike \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 4:1 & 0.22 & 0.18 & $+$0.10 & $+$0.13 & Flat \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$ & 2:1 & 0.23 & 0.18 & $+$0.14 & $+$0.16 & Flat \\\\\nPythia-1.4B & 1:1 & 0.24 & 0.12 & $+$10.66 & $+$10.82 & Flat \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe per-head heatmap (Figure~\\ref{fig:heatmap}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:heatmap_appendix}) confirms this visually: Qwen's Layer-0 heads show high distortion while mid-to-deep layers are near-zero (spread); Yi-1.5 shows a single bright spot at Layer-4 Head-3 (34\\% of total delta) with the remaining 191 heads near-uniform (spike); Gemma-2 and Mistral show uniformly low distortion.\n\n\\subsection{Empirical Decision Rule}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\nMCKP benefit requires not just high concentration but a specific \\emph{shape}---spread heterogeneity across multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients.\nThis motivates a joint empirical decision rule for deciding when non-uniform allocation is worthwhile.\nGiven the profiling results from \\S\\ref{sec:profiling}, compute the top-5\\% concentration and $t_1/t_5$ ratio, then select:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep]\n\\item \\textbf{Spread} (concentration $\\gtrsim 0.50$, $t_1/t_5 \\lesssim 0.70$): non-uniform recommended. Qwen (0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$): $-$29.4\\% PPL.\n\\item \\textbf{Spike} ($t_1/t_5 \\gtrsim 0.70$, concentration $\\gtrsim 0.25$): use uniform. A dominant outlier with no cross-bitwidth gradient cannot be exploited.     "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Heterogeneity Characterization}\n\\label{sec:heterogeneity}\n\nThe benefit of non-uniform allocation depends on how unevenly head sensitivities are distributed.\nWe quantify this with a \\emph{top-5\\% concentration metric}: sort per-head 2-bit distortions $d_h(2)$ in descending order and compute the fraction of total distortion contributed by the top 5\\% of heads.\nHigh concentration indicates that a few heads are disproportionately sensitive; low concentration indicates near-uniform sensitivity.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:heterogeneity} summarizes the results across seven models at $4\\times$ compression.\nThe central finding is that MCKP benefit requires \\emph{spread heterogeneity}---multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients---rather than sensitivity concentrated in a single outlier at one bitwidth.\n\nQwen2.5-7B exemplifies the spread pattern (top-5\\% concentration 0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$), with Layer-0 heads contributing the majority of total 2-bit distortion across multiple bitwidths.\nConstrained MCKP reduces degradation from $+$5.64 PPL (uniform) to $+$1.10 PPL, a 29.4\\% improvement over uniform 4-bit. %exp_id=qwen_constrained_mckp\n\nYi-1.5-9B is a critical counterexample: despite the highest concentration tested ($0.71$, exceeding Qwen's $0.61$), constrained MCKP matches uniform ($+$0.20 vs.\\ $+$0.20). %exp_id=yi15_hires_constrained_mckp\nYi-1.5's sensitivity is dominated by a single extreme outlier: Layer-4 Head-3 ($d_h(2){=}13.12$, second-ranked head: $0.55$, a $24\\times$ gap; $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$). %exp_id=yi15_hires_profiling\nCritically, L4H3's 4-bit and 8-bit deltas are ${\\approx}\\,0$, so upgrading it from 2-bit to 4-bit removes the distortion, but budget freed by downgrading other heads has nowhere useful to go---a ``spike'' pattern with sensitivity at only one bitwidth.\nIn contrast, Qwen's Layer-0 heads exhibit non-zero deltas at multiple bitwidths (e.g., L0H0: 2-bit delta ${=}6.74$, with positive 4-bit and 8-bit deltas), giving the solver a multi-bitwidth gradient across several heads---a ``spread'' pattern enabling meaningful reallocation. %exp_id=qwen_hires_profiling\n\nLlama-3.1-8B shows moderate concentration ($0.34$) with a spike pattern ($t_1/t_5{=}0.71$); its allocation is indistinguishable from uniform ($+$0.36 vs.\\ $+$0.38). %exp_id=llama_hires_bf16_2bitonly %exp_id=llama_hires_constrained\nMistral-7B-v0.3 ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) and Gemma-2-9B ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) both exhibit flat distributions with low concentration ($0.22$ and $0.23$), leaving no reliable signal; constrained MCKP is slightly worse than uniform in both cases ($+$0.13 vs.\\ $+$0.10 for Mistral; $+$0.16 vs.\\ $+$0.14 for Gemma-2). %exp_id=mistral_c4_cross_dataset %exp_id=gemma2_full_pipeline\n\nPythia-1.4B extends the evaluation to MHA (1:1 ratio, 384 heads).\nLow concentration ($0.24$) and $t_1/t_5{=}0.12$ place it in the flat category; constrained MCKP confirms this ($+$10.82 vs.\\ uniform $+$10.66, $+$0.7\\%). %exp_id=pythia14b_constrained_pipeline\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent concentration dilution.}\nQwen2.5-14B (GQA 5:1, 384 heads) provides a within-family comparison: $t_1/t_5$ is identical (0.57), but concentration drops from 0.61 (7B) to 0.33 (14B)---the same within-head structure, diluted by $3.4\\times$ more heads into the flat regime.\nSensitivity is dominated by a single head (L0H5, $d_h(2){=}1.845$; second-ranked: $0.237$, a $7.8\\times$ gap), but 384 heads dilute cumulative signal below the concentration threshold.\nConcentration, not within-head structure, is thus the primary discriminator: the rule correctly identifies Qwen-7B as the single model$\\times$scale combination where non-uniform allocation helps.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Head sensitivity heterogeneity and constrained allocation benefit at $4\\times$ compression. $t_1/t_5$: ratio of top-1 to top-5 per-head 2-bit distortion (low${=}$spread, high${=}$spike). --- = experiment pending. $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:heterogeneity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & GQA & Top-5\\% & $t_1/t_5$ & $\\Delta$ PPL & $\\Delta$ PPL & Shape \\\\\n & Ratio & Conc. & & (Uniform) & (Ours) & \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B & 7:1 & 0.61 & 0.57 & $+$5.64 & $+$1.10 & Spread \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & 5:1 & 0.33 & 0.57 & --- & --- & Flat \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B & 8:1 & 0.71 & 0.90 & $+$0.20 & $+$0.20 & Spike \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B & 4:1 & 0.34 & 0.71 & $+$0.38 & $+$0.36 & Spike \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 4:1 & 0.22 & 0.18 & $+$0.10 & $+$0.13 & Flat \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$ & 2:1 & 0.23 & 0.18 & $+$0.14 & $+$0.16 & Flat \\\\\nPythia-1.4B & 1:1 & 0.24 & 0.12 & $+$7.80 & $+$7.96 & Flat \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe per-head heatmap (Figure~\\ref{fig:heatmap}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:heatmap_appendix}) confirms this visually: Qwen's Layer-0 heads show high distortion while mid-to-deep layers are near-zero (spread); {
  "recorded_role": "user",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Heterogeneity Characterization}\n\\label{sec:heterogeneity}\n\nThe benefit of non-uniform allocation depends on how unevenly head sensitivities are distributed.\nWe quantify this with a \\emph{top-5\\% concentration metric}: sort per-head 2-bit distortions $d_h(2)$ in descending order and compute the fraction of total distortion contributed by the top 5\\% of heads.\nHigh concentration indicates that a few heads are disproportionately sensitive; low concentration indicates near-uniform sensitivity.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:heterogeneity} summarizes the results across seven models at $4\\times$ compression.\nThe central finding is that MCKP benefit requires \\emph{spread heterogeneity}---multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients---rather than sensitivity concentrated in a single outlier at one bitwidth.\n\nQwen2.5-7B exemplifies the spread pattern (top-5\\% concentration 0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$), with Layer-0 heads contributing the majority of total 2-bit distortion across multiple bitwidths.\nConstrained MCKP reduces degradation from $+$5.64 PPL (uniform) to $+$1.10 PPL, a 29.4\\% improvement over uniform 4-bit. %exp_id=qwen_constrained_mckp\n\nYi-1.5-9B is a critical counterexample: despite the highest concentration tested ($0.71$, exceeding Qwen's $0.61$), constrained MCKP matches uniform ($+$0.20 vs.\\ $+$0.20). %exp_id=yi15_hires_constrained_mckp\nYi-1.5's sensitivity is dominated by a single extreme outlier: Layer-4 Head-3 ($d_h(2){=}13.12$, second-ranked head: $0.55$, a $24\\times$ gap; $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$). %exp_id=yi15_hires_profiling\nCritically, L4H3's 4-bit and 8-bit deltas are ${\\approx}\\,0$, so upgrading it from 2-bit to 4-bit removes the distortion, but budget freed by downgrading other heads has nowhere useful to go---a ``spike'' pattern with sensitivity at only one bitwidth.\nIn contrast, Qwen's Layer-0 heads exhibit non-zero deltas at multiple bitwidths (e.g., L0H0: 2-bit delta ${=}6.74$, with positive 4-bit and 8-bit deltas), giving the solver a multi-bitwidth gradient across several heads---a ``spread'' pattern enabling meaningful reallocation. %exp_id=qwen_hires_profiling\n\nLlama-3.1-8B shows moderate concentration ($0.34$) with a spike pattern ($t_1/t_5{=}0.71$); its allocation is indistinguishable from uniform ($+$0.36 vs.\\ $+$0.38). %exp_id=llama_hires_bf16_2bitonly %exp_id=llama_hires_constrained\nMistral-7B-v0.3 ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) and Gemma-2-9B ($t_1/t_5{=}0.18$) both exhibit flat distributions with low concentration ($0.22$ and $0.23$), leaving no reliable signal; constrained MCKP is slightly worse than uniform in both cases ($+$0.13 vs.\\ $+$0.10 for Mistral; $+$0.16 vs.\\ $+$0.14 for Gemma-2). %exp_id=mistral_c4_cross_dataset %exp_id=gemma2_full_pipeline\n\nPythia-1.4B extends the evaluation to MHA (1:1 ratio, 384 heads).\nLow concentration ($0.24$) and $t_1/t_5{=}0.12$ place it in the flat category; constrained MCKP confirms this ($+$7.96 vs.\\ uniform $+$7.80, $+$0.7\\%). %exp_id=pythia14b_constrained_pipeline\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent concentration dilution.}\nQwen2.5-14B (GQA 5:1, 384 heads) provides a within-family comparison: $t_1/t_5$ is identical (0.57), but concentration drops from 0.61 (7B) to 0.33 (14B)---the same within-head structure, diluted by $3.4\\times$ more heads into the flat regime.\nSensitivity is dominated by a single head (L0H5, $d_h(2){=}1.845$; second-ranked: $0.237$, a $7.8\\times$ gap), but 384 heads dilute cumulative signal below the concentration threshold.\nConcentration, not within-head structure, is thus the primary discriminator: the rule correctly identifies Qwen-7B as the single model$\\times$scale combination where non-uniform allocation helps.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Head sensitivity heterogeneity and constrained allocation benefit at $4\\times$ compression. $t_1/t_5$: ratio of top-1 to top-5 per-head 2-bit distortion (low${=}$spread, high${=}$spike). --- = experiment pending. $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:heterogeneity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & GQA & Top-5\\% & $t_1/t_5$ & $\\Delta$ PPL & $\\Delta$ PPL & Shape \\\\\n & Ratio & Conc. & & (Uniform) & (Ours) & \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B & 7:1 & 0.61 & 0.57 & $+$5.64 & $+$1.10 & Spread \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & 5:1 & 0.33 & 0.57 & --- & --- & Flat \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B & 8:1 & 0.71 & 0.90 & $+$0.20 & $+$0.20 & Spike \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B & 4:1 & 0.34 & 0.71 & $+$0.38 & $+$0.36 & Spike \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 4:1 & 0.22 & 0.18 & $+$0.10 & $+$0.13 & Flat \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$ & 2:1 & 0.23 & 0.18 & $+$0.14 & $+$0.16 & Flat \\\\\nPythia-1.4B & 1:1 & 0.24 & 0.12 & $+$7.80 & $+$7.96 & Flat \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe per-head heatmap (Figure~\\ref{fig:heatmap}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:heatmap_appendix}) confirms this visually: Qwen's Layer-0 heads show high distortion while mid-to-deep layers are near-zero (spread); Yi-1.5 shows a single bright spot at Layer-4 Head-3 (34\\% of total delta) with the remaining 191 heads near-uniform (spike); Gemma-2 and Mistral show uniformly low distortion.\n\n\\subsection{Empirical Decision Rule}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\nMCKP benefit requires not just high concentration but a specific \\emph{shape}---spread heterogeneity across multiple heads with cross-bitwidth sensitivity gradients.\nThis motivates a joint empirical decision rule for deciding when non-uniform allocation is worthwhile.\nGiven the profiling results from \\S\\ref{sec:profiling}, compute the top-5\\% concentration and $t_1/t_5$ ratio, then select:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep]\n\\item \\textbf{Spread} (concentration $\\gtrsim 0.50$, $t_1/t_5 \\lesssim 0.70$): non-uniform recommended. Qwen (0.61, $t_1/t_5{=}0.57$): $-$29.4\\% PPL.\n\\item \\textbf{Spike} ($t_1/t_5 \\gtrsim 0.70$, concentration $\\gtrsim 0.25$): use uniform. A dominant outlier with no cross-bitwidth gradient cannot be exploited. Yi-1.5 (0.71, 0.90) and Llama (0.34, 0.71): no benefit.\n\\item \\textbf{Flat} (concentration $\\lesssim 0.25$, or remaining moderate-concentration models with $t_1/t_5 \\lesssim 0.70$): use uniform. Too little cumulative delta for meaningful reallocation. Mistral (0.22), Gemma-2 (0.23), Qwen-14B (0.33), and Pythia (0.24): no benefit or counterproductive.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nBoth conditions are needed: Yi-1.5's concentration (0.71) exceeds the 0.50 threshold but its spike shape ($t_1/t_5{=}0.90$) correctly predicts no benefit.\nA concentration-only threshold would misclassify it as spread.\nThe rule correctly classifies all seven evaluated models (7/7), including the within-family scale contrast (Qwen-7B spread vs.\\ 14B flat) and the MHA architecture (Pythia-1.4B flat).\nOnly Qwen-7B exhibits the spread pattern ($N{=}1$); the suggested thresholds should be treated as illustrative until confirmed on additional spread architectures.\nThe rule reuses profiling data already required by the solver and adds no overhead.\n\nAdditional analyses---granularity tradeoff (Appendix~\\ref{sec:granularity}), aggressive compression, profiling resolution and stability, per-layer optimality, and KV separation---are in Appendices~\\ref{sec:granularity}--\\ref{sec:kv_separation}.\n",

[Director 指令] [Patch] 新增 8K Long-context Spot-check 结果

**数据（加入 Experiments 或 Analysis 章节，1 个表 + 1 段讨论）：**

| Model | Shape | Method | 2K PPL | 8K PPL |
|-------|-------|--------|--------|--------|
| Qwen-7B | Spread | BF16 baseline | 9.81 | 6.97 |
| Qwen-7B | Spread | MCKP (k=8) | 10.89 | 8.59 |
| Qwen-7B | Spread | Uniform 4-bit | 15.45 | **77.75** |
| Llama-3.1-8B | Spike | BF16 baseline | 8.09 | 5.60 |
| Llama-3.1-8B | Spike | MCKP (k=2) | 8.50 | 6.05 |
| Llama-3.1-8B | Spike | Uniform 4-bit | 8.47 | 6.04 |

**核心叙事**：
1. Qwen (Spread): uniform 4-bit 在 8K 下从 1.57× baseline 退化到 11.15× baseline（catastrophic），MCKP 从 1.11× 仅增到 1.23×（robust）。MCKP improvement 从 80.8% → 97.7%。
2. Llama (Spike): 两种方法在 8K 下仍无差异（6.05 vs 6.04），与 2K 一致。
3. 结论：diagnostic (concentration + t1/t5) 在标准和长 context 下均有效，且 Spread 模型的 MCKP 优势随 context length 放大。

**建议位置**：Experiments 的 "Long-Context Generalization" 小节，或 Analysis 章节末尾。注明是 spot-check（Qwen 36 chunks, Llama 类似），profile 基于 2K calibration 但在 8K 仍有效（profile 泛化性正面证据）。

**不要引用 Yi 8K 数据**——Yi max_position=4096，8K 超出原生 context window，已排除。

这次不用验证引用。

```text
149	Joint allocation is sufficient under the current constraint formulation.
150	Separated K/V allocation with per-side cardinality constraints---limiting 2-bit assignments independently for K and V---is a direction for future work.
151	
152	\section{Runtime Benchmark}
153	\label{sec:runtime}
154	
155	\begin{table}[ht]
156	\centering
157	\caption{PyTorch-level latency on Qwen2.5-7B (single NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (96\,GB), prompt\_len=256, gen\_len=128, mean of 10 runs). All conditions store KV in bf16 with simulated per-head quantize/dequantize calls; peak GPU memory is identical across rows.}
158	\label{tab:runtime}
159	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
160	\toprule
161	Condition & Prefill (ms) & Decode (ms/tok) & Throughput (tok/s) \\
162	\midrule
163	Baseline bf16       & 45.86 & 33.78 & 29.6 \\
164	Uniform 4-bit       & 54.25 & 42.46 & 23.6 \\
165	Constrained MCKP    & 57.03 & 46.09 & 21.8 \\
166	\bottomrule
167	\end{tabular}
168	\end{table}
169	
170	Table~\ref{tab:runtime} reports end-to-end latency for three KV cache configurations.
171	Uniform 4-bit quantization adds 25.7\% decode overhead relative to the bf16 baseline (42.46 vs.\ 33.78~ms/tok), reflecting per-head quantize/dequantize calls at every attention step.
172	Constrained MCKP adds a further 8.5\% over uniform (46.09 vs.\ 42.46~ms/tok), because mixed-precision allocation dispatches different dequantization kernels for 2-bit, 4-bit, and 8-bit heads within the same layer.
173	The MCKP solver itself runs in ${\sim}5$~ms and is invoked once at initialization, contributing negligibly to end-to-end latency.
174	Both quantized conditions achieve the same $4\times$ KV cache compression (5.25~MB vs.\ 21~MB at bf16), so the latency difference between uniform and MCKP reflects only the mixed-precision dispatch overhead, not a memory trade-off.
175	
176	\paragraph{Caveat.}
177	These measurements use PyTorch-level per-head \texttt{quantize}/\texttt{dequantize} calls and do \emph{not} represent optimized CUDA kernel performance.
178	Production inference engines such as vLLM and TensorRT-LLM employ fused attention kernels that batch dequantization with the matrix multiply, amortizing the per-head dispatch cost that dominates the gap between uniform and MCKP in our simulation.
179	The 8.5\% overhead reported here should therefore be treated as an upper bound on the mixed-precision penalty; real-world deployment with fused kernels would likely narrow or eliminate this gap.
180	The limitation noted in Section~\ref{sec:conclusion}---that we report no real-kernel latency benchmarks---remains: the numbers above quantify simulation overhead, not production inference speedup.
181	
```

```text
175	
176	\paragraph{Caveat.}
177	These measurements use PyTorch-level per-head \texttt{quantize}/\texttt{dequantize} calls and do \emph{not} represent optimized CUDA kernel performance.
178	Production inference engines such as vLLM and TensorRT-LLM employ fused attention kernels that batch dequantization with the matrix multiply, amortizing the per-head dispatch cost that dominates the gap between uniform and MCKP in our simulation.
179	The 8.5\% overhead reported here should therefore be treated as an upper bound on the mixed-precision penalty; real-world deployment with fused kernels would likely narrow or eliminate this gap.
180	The limitation noted in Section~\ref{sec:conclusion}---that we report no real-kernel latency benchmarks---remains: the numbers above quantify simulation overhead, not production inference speedup.
181	
```

  "new_string": "The limitation noted in Section~\\ref{sec:conclusion}---that we report no real-kernel latency benchmarks---remains: the numbers above quantify simulation overhead, not production inference speedup.\n\n\\section{Long-Context Generalization}\n\\label{sec:long_context}\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{PPL at 2K and 8K context length on WikiText-2. Profiles calibrated at 2K (64 seq $\\times$ 2048 tok). Yi-1.5 excluded (max position 4096).}\n\\label{tab:long_context}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\nModel & Method & 2K PPL & 8K PPL \\\\\n\\midrule\n & BF16 baseline & 9.81 & 6.97 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & MCKP ($k{=}8$) & 10.89 & 8.59 \\\\\n & Uniform 4-bit & 15.45 & 77.75 \\\\\n\\midrule\n & BF16 baseline & 8.09 & 5.60 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B & MCKP ($k{=}2$) & 8.50 & 6.05 \\\\\n & Uniform 4-bit & 8.47 & 6.04 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:long_context} extends the evaluation to 8K context length using 2K-calibrated profiles.\nOn Qwen (spread), uniform 4-bit degrades from $1.57\\times$ bf16 at 2K to $11.15\\times$ at 8K---quantization errors accumulate catastrophically over longer sequences.\nConstrained MCKP remains robust ($1.11\\times$ at 2K, $1.23\\times$ at 8K), recovering 97.7\\% of the quantization loss at 8K vs.\\ 80.8\\% at 2K.\nOn Llama (spike), both methods remain indistinguishable at 8K (6.05 vs.\\ 6.04), consistent with the 2K result.\nThe diagnostic (concentration $+$ $t_1/t_5$) thus generalizes across context lengths, and the spread-pattern MCKP advantage amplifies with longer sequences.\nProfiles calibrated at 2K transfer effectively to 8K without re-profiling, providing positive evidence of calibration generalization."
}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PFNqw6VgGtijXuLVGxjj5J`</summary>

```text
109	On Qwen, constrained MCKP improves average 0-shot accuracy by 2.6 pts over uniform (0.686 vs.\ 0.660), recovering $\sim$73\% of the accuracy lost to quantization.
110	The four non-spread architectures show minor average degradation (Yi $-$0.8, Llama $-$1.0, Mistral $-$0.5, Gemma-2 $+$0.1 pts); Llama is the most systematic, with 0 of 5 tasks showing positive change.
111	ARC-Challenge shows the largest per-task sensitivity across all models (Yi $-$3.2, Llama $-$2.7, Mistral $-$1.2, Gemma-2 $-$0.2 pts), consistent with multi-step reasoning where KV cache errors accumulate across longer inference chains.
112	Cross-dataset stability analysis (Appendix~\ref{sec:cross_dataset}) confirms ${>}96\%$ bit-assignment agreement between WikiText-2 and C4 calibration across all three tested models.
```

</details>

    "newString": "Cross-dataset stability analysis (Appendix~\\ref{sec:cross_dataset}) confirms ${>}96\\%$ bit-assignment agreement between WikiText-2 and C4 calibration across all three tested models.\nA long-context spot-check (Appendix~\\ref{sec:long_context}) shows that on Qwen at 8K tokens, uniform 4-bit degrades catastrophically ($11.15\\times$ bf16) while constrained MCKP remains robust ($1.23\\times$); on Llama, both methods stay indistinguishable.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\n\\paragraph{Models.}\nWe evaluate seven models spanning five architecture families and diverse attention configurations:\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-7B} (GQA 7:1, 28 layers $\\times$ 4 KV heads = 112),\n\\textbf{Qwen2.5-14B} (GQA 5:1, 48 $\\times$ 8 = 384),\n\\textbf{Yi-1.5-9B} (GQA 8:1, 48 $\\times$ 4 = 192),\n\\textbf{Llama-3.1-8B} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256),\n\\textbf{Mistral-7B-v0.3} (GQA 4:1, 32 $\\times$ 8 = 256, sliding window attention),\n\\textbf{Gemma-2-9B} (GQA 2:1, 42 $\\times$ 8 = 336, \\texttt{head\\_dim}=256, attention logit softcapping),\nand \\textbf{Pythia-1.4B} (MHA 1:1, 24 $\\times$ 16 = 384).\nThis selection spans GQA ratios from 1:1 (MHA) to 8:1 and covers standard, sliding-window, softcapped, and multi-head attention.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation.}\nWe report perplexity (PPL, $\\downarrow$) on the WikiText-2 test split (298{,}938 tokens) under bf16 inference with stride 512.\nFor downstream evaluation, we use ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, PIQA, and WinoGrande under 0-shot prompting.\n\n\\paragraph{Profiling.}\nPer-head sensitivity is measured by independently quantizing each head to each candidate bit-width in $\\{2, 4, 8, 16\\}$ and recording the PPL increase over the bf16 baseline.\nAll results use high-resolution profiling (64 sequences $\\times$ 2048 tokens from WikiText-2 train); Appendix~\\ref{sec:profiling_resolution} compares against standard-resolution profiling (16 seq $\\times$ 512 tok).\n\n\\paragraph{Compression and constraint.}\nThe default target is 4$\\times$ compression (average 4 bits, budget ratio 0.25).\nThe cardinality constraint limits 2-bit assignments to at most $k$ heads, where $k$ is selected via the compounding sweep described in \\S\\ref{sec:method} ($k{=}8$ for Qwen-7B, $k{=}2$ for Yi-1.5 and Llama, $k{=}4$ for Mistral, $k{=}8$ for Gemma-2 and Pythia; Qwen-14B $k$ value pending).\n\n\\paragraph{Baselines.}\nWe compare against:\n(1)~uniform 4-bit quantization;\n(2)~unconstrained greedy allocation (RateQuant-style~\\cite{zuo2026ratequant});\n(3)~unconstrained MCKP allocation;\n(4)~an L0-random heuristic that assigns Layer-0 heads to 8-bit and $k$ random non-Layer-0 heads to 2-bit, with remaining heads at 4-bit (5 seeds; we report mean$\\pm$std and median);\nand (5)~a naive positional heuristic that assigns Layer-0 to 8-bit and the $k$ deepest heads to 2-bit.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main_results}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{PPL ($\\downarrow$) on WikiText-2 at 4$\\times$ compression. Unconstrained failure: catastrophic on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), severe on Pythia ($1.9\\times$), moderate on Gemma-2 ($1.5\\times$), mild on remaining (${\\sim}1.1\\times$). Greedy and MCKP produce identical unconstrained allocations on 4/5 models. Best constrained result per model in \\textbf{bold}; --- = experiment pending. $^\\ddagger$Best $k$ from ablation ($k{=}4$). $^*$BOS-corrected.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & Baseline & Uniform & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Unconstrained} & Ours \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){4-5}\n & (bf16) & (4-bit) & Greedy & MCKP & (Constr.) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B      & 9.81  & 15.45 & 32.45 & 36.92 & \\textbf{10.91} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B     & 5.84  & ---   & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B       & 7.02  & 7.22  & 7.56  & 7.56  & 7.22 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B    & 8.09  & 8.47  & 8.63  & 8.63  & \\textbf{8.45} \\\\\nMistral-7B-v0.3 & 7.04  & 7.13  & 7.57  & 7.60  & 7.16$^\\ddagger$ \\\\\nGemma-2-9B$^*$  & 6.03  & 6.17  & 8.85  & 8.85  & 6.20 \\\\\nPythia-1.4B     & 16.49 & 24.29 & ---   & 31.54 & 24.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen2.5-7B, constrained MCKP achieves 10.91 PPL, a 29.4\\% reduction over uniform 4-bit (15.45).\nWithout the constraint, both greedy (32.45) and MCKP (36.92) perform \\emph{worse} than uniform---a catastrophic failure from excessive 2-bit assignments compounding across layers.\nAcross 3 calibration seeds, constrained MCKP yields mean PPL 10.99$\\pm$0.06 with 90.2\\% three-way allocation agreement, confirming profiling stability.\n\nOn Llama-3.1-8B, the result is essentially indistinguishable from uniform (8.45 vs.\\ 8.47, $\\Delta < 0.02$ PPL).\nHires profiling reveals a spike pattern (concentration 0.34, $t_1/t_5{=}0.71$) dominated by a single top head (L2H5), leaving little cross-bitwidth gradient for non-uniform allocation to exploit.\n\nOn Yi-1.5-9B, constrained MCKP ($k{=}2$) matches uniform (7.22); despite the highest concentration tested (0.71), sensitivity is dominated by a single outlier (L4H3, $t_1/t_5{=}0.90$), a spike pattern with no cross-bitwidth gradient.\nOn Mistral-7B-v0.3, constrained MCKP (7.16, $k{=}4$) is slightly worse than uniform (7.13, $+$0.4\\%); near-zero sensitivity differences and sliding-window attention may both contribute.\n\nOn Gemma-2-9B$^*$ (GQA 2:1), constrained MCKP ($k{=}8$) achieves 6.20 PPL vs.\\ uniform 6.17 ($+$0.4\\%).\nDespite flat sensitivity (concentration 0.23), unconstrained MCKP degrades to 8.85 ($1.47\\times$ bf16) from 31.5\\% 2-bit over-allocation; the constraint reduces this to 8 heads, confirming universal over-allocation prevention.\n\nUnconstrained failure severity tracks heterogeneity shape: \\emph{catastrophic} on Qwen ($3.3\\times$ bf16), \\emph{severe} on Pythia ($1.9\\times$, 90/384 heads at 2-bit), \\emph{moderate} on Gemma-2 ($1.47\\times$, 31.5\\% 2-bit), and \\emph{mild} on the remaining models (${\\sim}1.07$--$1.08\\times$).\nSee Appendix~\\ref{sec:allocation_appendix} (allocation visualization) and Appendix~\\ref{sec:budget_sweep} (budget sweep) for additional detail.\n\nMCKP consistently outperforms random L0-constrained allocation and naive positional baselines across all three tested models (median gap 5--16\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:heuristic} in Appendix~\\ref{sec:heuristic_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Comparison with KIVI.}\nKIVI~\\cite{liu2024kivi} uses asymmetric per-channel quantization with per-token scaling, applied uniformly across all heads.\nAt 4-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI achieves near-lossless PPL (9.91 on Qwen, 8.14 on Llama vs.\\ 9.81/8.09 bf16), while our symmetric scheme yields 15.45 and 8.47---a gap from the quantization method, not allocation.\nHowever, KIVI cannot exploit head-level heterogeneity: on Qwen (spread), constrained MCKP reduces symmetric 4-bit from 15.45 to 10.91 ($-$4.54 PPL) by reallocating bits across heads, a degree of freedom unavailable to uniform-precision methods; on Llama (spike), the gain is negligible ($-$0.02).\nAt 2-bit ($r{=}0$), KIVI degrades further (13.34 on Qwen, 10.08 on Llama) as sensitive heads receive the same precision as insensitive ones.\nThe two approaches are orthogonal and composable: KIVI selects the per-channel scheme, our method selects the per-head bit-width.\n\n\\subsection{Downstream Evaluation}\n\\label{sec:downstream}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{0-shot accuracy change (Ours $-$ Uniform, pts) at 4$\\times$ compression. Qwen shows large gains; the remaining models show small degradations or neutrality, tracking PPL results.}\n\\label{tab:downstream}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & ARC-C & HS & PIQA & WG & Avg \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-7B        & $+$3.2 & $+$4.0 & $+$1.3 & $+$2.0 & $+$2.6 \\\\\nYi-1.5-9B        & $-$3.2 & $-$0.6 & $-$0.3 & $+$1.3 & $-$0.8 \\\\\nLlama-3.1-8B     & $-$2.7 & $-$0.1 & $-$0.7 & $-$0.2 & $-$1.0 \\\\\nMistral-7B       & $-$1.2 & $-$0.2 & $+$0.4 & $-$1.0 & $-$0.5 \\\\\nGemma-2-9B       & $-$0.2 & $+$0.3 & $-$0.1 & $+$0.8 & $+$0.1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nOn Qwen, constrained MCKP improves average 0-shot accuracy by 2.6 pts over uniform (0.686 vs.\\ 0.660), recovering $\\sim$73\\% of the accuracy lost to quantization.\nThe four non-spread architectures show minor average degradation (Yi $-$0.8, Llama $-$1.0, Mistral $-$0.5, Gemma-2 $+$0.1 pts); Llama is the most systematic, with 0 of 5 tasks showing positive change.\nARC-Challenge shows the largest per-task sensitivity across all models (Yi $-$3.2, Llama $-$2.7, Mistral $-$1.2, Gemma-2 $-$0.2 pts), consistent with multi-step reasoning where KV cache errors accumulate across longer inference chains.\nCross-dataset stability analysis (Appendix~\\ref{sec:cross_dataset}) confirms ${>}96\\%$ bit-assignment agreement between WikiText-2 and C4 calibration across all three tested models.",
